| UI-MODEL-GATING-01              | High     | Fixed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Desktop + unified-chat         | `apps/desktop/src/stores/appModeStore.ts`, `packages/ui/unified-chat/src/components/ModelSelector.tsx`, `packages/ui/unified-chat/src/components/ChatInput.tsx`, `apps/desktop/src/App.tsx`                                                                                                                      | Production path is `DesktopShellV3` → unified-chat `ChatInterface` → unified-chat `ModelSelector` (the appMode-gated `v3/ModelPopover.tsx` is dead code — rendered nowhere). `ModelSelector` IS mode-aware: it reads the desktop mode via `readPersistedDesktopMode()` (`localStorage['app-mode-store'].state.mode`) and, in Local mode, shows local/BYOK only. Root cause of the cloud catalog leaking into Local mode: zustand `persist` lazy-writes only on the first `set()`, so a session that never toggled left `app-mode-store` ABSENT → reader returned null → `isDesktopLocalMode=false` → `usingFallback=true` → `CLOUD_FALLBACK_MODELS` shown with a "13+ Providers" badge in Local mode (trust-boundary-confusing label; egress guard still blocked the call). Fix: prime persistence on first load in `appModeStore.ts` (one-time no-op `setState` when the key is absent) so the mode is written and cross-package readers detect Local mode. Follow-up (no-model send) fixed: when `selectedModelId===''` (no usable local/BYOK model), `ChatInput` now disables the Send button (greyed), guards `handleSend`, and shows a "Select a model to start" placeholder, so a no-model send can no longer silently create a dead conversation. Cloud auto-routing (`auto-*`, non-empty) stays enabled. `mode:'auto'`. LIVE CONFIRMATION 2026-07-03 (desktop-lifecycle-qa): drove a real agentic send via WDIO asking the model to run `terminal_execute`. Backend log proves the REAL enforcement point is the separate Rust `AgentMode` (`sys/commands/tool_confirmation.rs`, default `AgentMode::Safe`) driven by `set_agent_mode` — NOT the composer's visible "Ask before edits"/`agentControlStore` control, which has no effect on actual tool permission. Confirmed real backend log: `Tool 'terminal_execute' is not permitted in safe mode` (correctly blocked, `elapsed_ms=13`). The only reachable UI for `set_agent_mode` is `SafetyPolicies.tsx` (Settings, not the composer) — `AgentModeSwitcher.tsx` (the Safe/Plan/Build/Autopilot 4-button control) is dead code, confirmed via grep: it is never imported/rendered anywhere in `apps/desktop/src` outside its own definition file. Net effect: a fresh user sees the composer defaulting to "Edit automatically" (implying tools will run), but EVERY confirmation-gated tool (terminal, file writes, etc.) is silently blocked by the real backend gate defaulting to Safe, with no path to change it except digging into Settings > (whichever tab hosts `SafetyPolicies`) — worse than the already-tracked drift, since the visible control is actively misleading about what will happen. |
| LOCAL-CHAT-NOINVOKE-01          | Critical | Fixed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Desktop + tauri + unified-chat | `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sys/commands/chat/send_message.rs`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sys/commands/chat/send_message_setup.rs`, `apps/desktop/src/runtime/TauriRuntime.ts`, `packages/ui/unified-chat/src/hooks/useChat.ts`                                                                                | In the REAL Tauri desktop app (Local mode, real local Ollama backend), sending a chat renders the user bubble but NEVER invokes the local model. Ollama's `~/.ollama/logs/server.log` shows only `/api/tags` polling — NO `/api/chat` / `/api/generate` — across 3+ sends and an app reload; `ollama ps` empty; no in-flight conn to 11434. Result: no response, no error, no loading indicator (silent failure). The web dev-local MOCK hid this completely — only the real Tauri+Ollama app revealed it. Frontend path verified correct: `getCurrentUserId()` returns synthetic `LOCAL_DESKTOP_USER_ID` in Local mode (sign-in guard passes); `TauriRuntime._streamMessage` invokes `chat_send_message` with `modelOverride` + `activeMode:'local'`. ONE gap fixed: `useChat` did not forward the model's `provider`, so `resolve_provider_and_model` received `provider=None` for a dynamic Ollama model (`gemma4:e4b`) absent from the static `models.json` catalog — `useChat.ts` now passes `provider`. That alone did NOT restore inference (still no `/api/chat` after reload), so the root cause is DEEPER in the Rust `chat_send_message` → provider/router → Ollama path (or `chat_create_conversation`). **UPDATE 2026-06-25 — full static trace (code-complete; needs runtime confirm; do NOT mark Fixed on compile alone — a prior plausible fix also compiled clean and did not fire `/api/chat`):** Traced the entire path frontend→backend and it is now CORRECT end-to-end. (1) Discovered Ollama models ARE merged into the unified-chat model store with `provider:'ollama'` — `App.tsx:625-642` calls `llm_list_ollama_models` and merges when `llm_get_available_models` returns no ollama — so `useChat.ts:428` resolves the provider and forwards it. (2) `resolve_provider_and_model` maps `provider='ollama'`→`Some(Provider::Ollama)` (`Provider::from_string`, `core/llm/mod.rs:710`); model passes through. (3) `handle_streaming_message:47` calls `ensure_ollama_provider` when `is_local_mode` (registers Ollama BEFORE routing so `candidates()` isn't empty — its own comment documents this exact silent-drop fix). (4) `candidates()` builds the explicit `{Ollama, model}` candidate (`has_provider`=present && `is_configured()`=`!base_url.is_empty()`=true), survives the `is_available()` `/api/version` filter, and `invoke_streaming_with_retry`→`provider.send_message_streaming`→`/api/chat`. **HOWEVER this is NOT "fixed" — the static trace found no defect, but the recorded runtime says still-broken WITH these fixes already in place.** `provider_access.rs` (`ensure_ollama_provider`) was committed 2026-06-23 (`d10e9ee18`), BEFORE this 2026-06-24 entry recorded `/api/chat` still not firing after reload — so the internally-consistent path above is the SAME code observed broken. That narrows the cause to RUNTIME-only, none statically provable: (a) `OllamaProvider::new(None)` failing at runtime (logged `warn!`, unsurfaced → empty candidates → the new loud Local error now fires instead of silence); (b) an async/lock race between `ensure_ollama_provider` (write lock) and `candidates()` (read lock); (c) `chat_create_conversation`/`prepare_send_message` erroring before the stream spawns; (d) a frontend timing issue where `modelState.models` is empty/stale at send time so `useChat.ts:428` yields `undefined`. STATUS: UNRESOLVED — runtime diagnosis required (the prior plausible fix also traced clean and did not fire `/api/chat`). This turn's value is NOT a fix: a regression test locking the front-of-chain invariant + the loud Local-mode empty-candidate error that converts the silent failure into a diagnosable one + this narrowed suspect list. Added: regression test `send_message_setup::tests::local_ollama_dynamic_model_resolves_to_ollama_provider` (locks the front-of-chain invariant, passes). Added defense-in-depth: the empty-candidate error in `llm_router.rs` (`route_with_retry` + `send_message_streaming_with_retry`) is now Local-specific ("No local model provider is reachable…") instead of the misleading "No LLM providers configured", so any residual drop is diagnosable not silent. RESIDUAL runtime suspects if it STILL drops: (a) `App.tsx:627` skips the merge if `llm_get_available_models` returns ANY ollama model — a partial list could omit the selected dynamic model; (b) `OllamaProvider::new(None)` failing at runtime (logged `warn!`, unsurfaced); (c) the frontend not rendering the emitted `chat:stream-end` error. **UPDATE 2026-07-03 — RE-CONFIRMED STILL BROKEN, live WDIO repro, now with a loud UI error (progress from silent failure, but underlying bug unchanged):** Real WDIO session (`wdio/specs/ai-response-rendering.spec.ts`), Local mode, model picker correctly opened and `qwen3.5:9b` selected (confirmed via `MODEL_SELECT_CLICKED: true` and DOM state), sent a real prompt. Result: assistant bubble renders the text "I couldn't generate a response. Please try again." (a real, explicit, user-facing error — not silence, so the earlier defense-in-depth loud-error work is functioning), and `~/.ollama/logs/server.log` confirmed still ONLY `/api/tags` GETs, zero `/api/chat` — identical to the original 2026-06-24 finding. Re-tested with `gemma4:e4b` (the original repro model) — same exact failure and error text, so this is NOT model-specific; it is a systemic Local-mode send-path break affecting every Ollama model. This blocks live-WDIO verification of Tier-1 checklist row #7 (AI Response Rendering): the WDIO harness itself now works (see `DESKTOP-WDIO-COMPOSER-MOUNT-FLAKY-01`, Fixed) and correctly drives model-selection + composer + send, but no assistant markdown can be produced to inspect because generation itself fails before any content streams back. **UPDATE 2026-07-03 (later same session) — ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED via live stderr instrumentation, real ACTUAL fix, not a static trace:** Added temporary `tracing::error!` logging around the exact `self.client.post(...).send().await` call in `ollama.rs` and inside the stream-consumer loop (`stream_runtime.rs`), then drove a real send via WDIO. This revealed TWO distinct, compounding bugs, both now fixed: (1) **Extended thinking not actually disabled** (`send_message_setup.rs::resolve_thinking_parameter`): when the frontend explicitly disables thinking (`thinking_mode == Some(false)`), the function returned `None` ("no preference") instead of `Some(ThinkingParameter::Enabled(false))` ("explicitly off"). For Anthropic/OpenAI, `None` correctly means "no extended thinking" by provider default. But Ollama's newer reasoning models (`qwen3.5`) default extended thinking ON at the API level — omitting the parameter does NOT disable it. `OllamaRequest` also had no `think` field at all, so even a correct signal had nowhere to go. Fix: return `Some(Enabled(false))` from the resolver, add `think: Option<bool>` to `OllamaRequest`, and a `resolve_ollama_think()` helper in `ollama.rs` that forwards it. Verified via direct `curl .../api/chat` with `"think":false` vs without: without it, the model spent 20+ seconds emitting only `message.thinking` (a field the app's SSE parser correctly does NOT surface as response content) before ever reaching `message.content`; with it, real content starts immediately. (2) **Context window (`num_ctx`) never set, defaulting to Ollama's own 4096**, regardless of the model's real capability (qwen3.5 advertises 128K). AGI Workforce's "Claude Desktop-like" chat mode injects a large system prompt plus every enabled MCP tool's JSON schema (111 tools) into every request; in a real repro this alone (plus accumulated conversation history) consumed 4095-8191 of a 4096/8192-token window, leaving the model ~1 token of budget before Ollama truncated with `done_reason:"length"` — producing exactly one character of real output (confirmed via chunk-level logging: `content="#" done=false` immediately followed by `content="" done=true finish_reason=Some("length")`, with `prompt_tokens` exactly equal to `num_ctx - 1` in every measurement). Fix: added `num_ctx: Option<u32>` to `OllamaOptions`, set to `OLLAMA_DEFAULT_NUM_CTX = 32768` (a fixed, generous default; a real per-model sizing from capability metadata is a tracked follow-up, not done here). Verified via `ollama ps` showing `CONTEXT 32768` loaded. Both fixes verified end-to-end with a real WDIO send (`wdio/specs/ai-response-rendering.spec.ts`): real markdown response generated and rendered (bold/italic/inline-code/fenced-code-block/table/blockquote/list all present in the live DOM), spec passes. Also discovered in passing (logged separately): `DESKTOP-MARKDOWN-NO-HEADING-01` — the renderer has zero markdown heading support. Also bumped `wdio.conf.ts`'s global mocha timeout 120s→300s (a real LLM generation call needs more headroom than typical UI-interaction specs; per-test overrides remain supported for outliers). | Repro: real Tauri app, Local mode, model `gemma4:e4b`, send any prompt → no `/api/chat` in `~/.ollama/logs/server.log`; UI shows user bubble, no reply. RUNTIME CONFIRM (the one remaining step): launch `tauri dev` capturing stderr, send in Local mode with an Ollama model, then `grep /api/chat ~/.ollama/logs/server.log` (now present?) and `grep -iE 'No local model\|Ollama\|streaming' stderr.log`. Regression test passes; crate compiles.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

A leading space (no command typed yet) is still ignored. Preserves the review-before-run design — no caller change. | UI-CLI-STATUSBAR-MODE-01 | Medium | Fixed | CLI | `apps/cli/src/tui/tui_app.rs` | The status-bar access chip showed only the active model's provider tier (`provider_access_mode`) — e.g. `◉ byok` — and never the SESSION privacy mode. In Local mode with a BYOK/Cloud model (the default state: session privacy defaults to Local, persisted per-project), the chip read "byok" while the session was actually Local and sends were BLOCKED, so users couldn't tell what mode they were in or why sends were refused. Fix: thread `session.privacy_mode` into `FrameCtx` (both construction sites; pre-capture `turn_privacy_mode` before the send future's mutable borrow to avoid E0503), and when `privacy_mode==Local && access_mode!=Local` (the only blocking combo per `validate_privacy_boundary`) render `◉ local≠{tier}` in danger color instead of the plain tier label. Aligned states unchanged (`◉ local`/`◉ byok`/`◉ cloud`). | Manual tmux REPL: `agi -m gemini-3.1-flash-lite` (Local session, byok model) → chip `◉ local≠byok` (danger); `agi -m tinyllama -p ollama` (aligned) → plain `◉ local` (green). `cargo build` clean. |

| BYOK-HANDOFF-CONSENT-01 | Critical | Fixed | CLI | `apps/cli/src/claude_parity.rs`, `apps/cli/src/agent/mod.rs`, `apps/cli/src/agent/chat.rs` | Trust-boundary leak (introduced by an earlier loop-fix, caught by the cli-bug-sweep workflow): `/continue-with-byok` (and `/byok`, `/fork-byok`) called `set_privacy_mode(provider_privacy_mode())` at command-issue time, so the session left Local mode the instant the draft was _created_ — before the user reviewed or sent it. A subsequent unrelated message then routed to BYOK silently, with none of the draft's secret-scan / preview / consent protections (violates the "never silently route Local→BYOK" rule). Fix: drafting no longer flips the mode; `arm_byok_handoff(&draft)` records the draft's BYOK preamble, and `consume_byok_handoff(user_input)` (called in the single `send()` chokepoint before `validate_privacy_boundary`) completes the Local→BYOK transition ONLY when the sent message carries that preamble — an unrelated Local message leaves the boundary intact and still blocks. Disclosure is the status chip flipping `◉ local≠byok`→`◉ byok`. | Unit test `byok_handoff_consents_only_on_matching_draft_send` (negative: unrelated msg → no flip, still blocks; positive: preamble → flip, clears). Manual tmux: `/continue-with-byok` keeps chip `local≠byok` (arm doesn't flip); sending the draft flips to `byok` past the boundary. 1699 lib tests pass. |
| UI-CLI-PICKER-LOCAL-01 | Medium | Fixed | CLI | `apps/cli/src/tui/tui_app.rs`, `apps/cli/src/tui/widgets/model_picker.rs` | The `/model` picker built its list from `catalog().all()` + cached OpenRouter models only — it never merged `local_models::discovered_models()`, so installed Ollama/LM Studio models (the local-first wedge) were absent from the picker even though `agi models scan`/`list` showed them. Fix: a process-global cache populated by a non-blocking background probe at TUI startup (avoids the 2.5s LM-Studio probe timeout blocking launch), merged into the picker's model list in the sync `/model` handler; discovered models map to `Model{provider:"ollama"}` → `ProviderId::Ollama` → the Local section. Also fixed `0K ctx` → `—` for models with unknown context (Ollama `/api/tags` omits it). | Manual tmux: `agi -m gemma4:e4b` → `/model` shows "Local · on-device · private · free" with all 5 Ollama models, current marked `●`. |
| UI-CLI-EFFORT-REASONING-01 | High | Fixed | CLI | `apps/cli/src/tui/widgets/model_picker.rs` | The `/model` picker showed the reasoning-effort selector for NON-reasoning models (e.g. claude-haiku-4-5) because `show_effort_bar()` gated on the provider's `supports_effort` (all Anthropic = true) instead of the highlighted model's `supports_reasoning` (SSOT `capabilities.thinking`). Fix: require `selected_model().supports_reasoning && provider.supports_effort`. (Separate, still-open: the effort selection is also a dead control — opens at Medium, never applies; tracked from the sweep.) | Unit test `effort_bar_depends_on_model_reasoning_not_just_provider` (haiku hidden, sonnet shown). Manual tmux: picker on haiku shows no effort footer. |

| UI-CLI-OVERLAY-DOUBLEBORDER-01 | Medium | Fixed | CLI | `apps/cli/src/tui/tui_app.rs` | Every `InteractiveView` overlay routed through `render_overlay` (command palette, diff-review, approval, elicitation) rendered a DOUBLE border: each widget's `render()` already emits a complete ASCII box (e.g. `┌─ Commands ─┐ … └─┘`), and `render_overlay` wrapped that already-boxed text again in a ratatui `Block::borders(ALL)`. Standalone pickers (model/effort/theme/agent) escaped it by drawing their own ratatui borders and bypassing `render_overlay`. Fix: size the overlay `Rect` to the widget's own content (max line width × line count) and render the text with no outer `Block` border, so the widget's box is the single frame (title preserved). | Manual tmux: `/` opens the command palette as a single `┌─ Commands ─┐` box (was box-in-box). 1699 lib tests pass (snapshots test widget `render()` in isolation, unaffected). |
| DESKTOP-WDIO-COMPOSER-MOUNT-FLAKY-01 | High | Fixed | Desktop QA infra | `apps/desktop/wdio/`, `apps/desktop/wdio/specs/smoke.spec.ts` | The React app intermittently fails to fully mount inside WDIO/embedded-webdriver sessions: `document.title` correctly shows "AGI" (native window/Tauri layer fine) but `document.body.innerHTML` stays at the static ~103-char shell (`<div id="root"></div>` + script tag, React never rendered into it), so any selector for in-app UI (e.g. `textarea[aria-label="Chat message input"]`) times out. Confirmed NOT specific to a new spec: the pre-existing, previously-reliable baseline `smoke.spec.ts` fails identically ("should launch and render a non-empty document" passes, "should show the chat composer" fails after 15s). Ruled out: the earlier, separate Vite port/504 dependency-scan deadlock (fixed this session by killing stale vite processes + `rm -rf node_modules/.vite` + clean restart) — confirmed the fix held via `/@vite/client` and a `.vite/deps` module both returning 200 (not just `/` or the entry file, which can falsely 200 while the dep graph is still broken), yet `smoke.spec.ts` still failed the same way afterward. Root cause NOT YET FOUND, but ruled out several candidates with direct evidence (2026-07-03 follow-up): (1) NOT a timing issue — polled `document.body.innerHTML.length`/`document.readyState`/`window.location.href` every 3s for 60s straight; `readyState` reaches `"complete"` within ~3s and `href` correctly resolves to `http://127.0.0.1:5173/`, but `innerHTML.length` stays pinned at 103 for the entire 60s, so a longer wait does not fix it. (2) NOT concurrent-process contention in this instance — `ps aux` at the time of the failing run showed zero other `agiworkforce-desktop`/wdio/webdriver processes running (only two long-lived shared `vite` dev-server processes; one, PID 19729 from 11:57AM, is stale and bound to port 5175, unrelated to this app's `devUrl` of 5173 — worth killing for shared-hygiene but was not the active cause here). (3) Separately discovered: `captureFrontendLogs: true` (wdio.conf.ts) has NEVER produced a single "Frontend"-tagged line in ANY `apps/desktop/logs/wdio-*.log` file in this project's history (checked all files) — frontend console/error capture in this WDIO+embedded-webdriver setup may itself be non-functional, independent of whether the app mounts; this removes "check the console for a JS error" as a currently-usable diagnostic until that capture path is fixed. Leading remaining suspects for the next agent: (a) the WKWebView-based embedded driver's module script (`<script type="module" src="/src/main.tsx">`) failing to execute or hanging silently in this specific webview/Tauri combination; (b) a CSP/webview security policy blocking the Vite dev module graph in the embedded-webdriver context specifically (never verified against the same devUrl in a normal browser tab); (c) the frontend log capture pipe being broken end-to-end such that even a real crash would look silent. This blocked live-WDIO verification of Tier-1 checklist row #7 (AI Response Rendering) this session — code-trace findings for that row were still recorded (see `desktop-qa-checklist.md` row 7), but live DOM-driven verification was not possible. Methodology note: do not conclude Vite is healthy from `/` or the entry file returning 200 alone; check `/@vite/client` and an actual `.vite/deps/*` module. | Repro: run `npx wdio run wdio.conf.ts --spec wdio/specs/smoke.spec.ts` from `apps/desktop/` — first assertion passes, second ("chat composer displayed") fails with `element (...) still not displayed after 15000ms`, even on a clean single-process Vite server with confirmed-200 client+deps endpoints. UPDATE 2026-07-03: no longer intermittent in this environment — reproduced 100% (4+ consecutive runs) across THREE different specs (`smoke.spec.ts`, a 60s-polling debug spec, `settings-tour.spec.ts`), the latter failing on both `nav[aria-label="Settings sections"]` and other selectors, i.e. this currently blocks essentially all live-UI WDIO verification for every agent on this checklist, not an occasional flake. ROOT CAUSE FOUND 2026-07-03 (both team-lead leads ruled out first: reverting the uncommitted `capabilities: ["default"]` line in tauri.conf.json did NOT fix it — that file is the only capability file so the restriction was a no-op; setting `csp: null` also did NOT fix it, ruling out CSP as a category entirely). Actual cause found via `browser.executeAsync` dynamically re-importing `/src/main.tsx` inside the live WDIO session: it rejected with WebKit's generic `"Importing a module script failed."` A full recursive crawl of the module graph (738+ files) found ~20 dependency chunks returning HTTP 504 — not core deps like `react.js`/`react-dom` (already warmed from prior curl checks, which is why the earlier `/@vite/client` + one-dep health check falsely looked fine) but LAZILY-DISCOVERED ones only pulled in by the real app's later imports: `@tauri-apps/api/{core,event,window,path}`, `@tauri-apps/plugin-{dialog,shell,updater,process,deep-link,fs}`, `mermaid`, `react-markdown`, `react-syntax-highlighter` (+ its prism styles chunk), `recharts`, `fuse.js`, several `@radix-ui/react-*` components, and `@wdio/tauri-plugin`. Vite's dependency optimizer had only pre-bundled the deps touched by my earlier manual curl checks, not the full graph the real app needs, and was stuck mid-re-scan for the rest (the same "outdated request" 504 pattern as the original port-contention issue, just recurring on a different/larger dep set). FIX: full clean restart (kill all vite processes including a stale, previously-undetected second instance on port 5175 + `rm -rf node_modules/.vite`) followed by a scripted crawl of the entire `/src/main.tsx` import graph (recursively following `from "..."` / `import(...)` specifiers) to force-touch every chunk until Vite's optimizer settled to 0 bad responses (808 files, all 200) BEFORE running WDIO — a plain `/@vite/client` + single-dep check is insufficient to confirm the dev server is actually ready. Re-verified `smoke.spec.ts` passing 3x in a row after the fix. Methodology update for future agents: after any Vite restart, warm the dependency optimizer by crawling the real app's module graph (or simply loading the app once in an actual browser tab and letting it settle) before trusting a WDIO run — checking 1-2 endpoints is not sufficient. |

| UI-CLI-TOOLDROP-SILENT-01 | High | Fixed | CLI | `apps/cli/src/tui/mod.rs`, `apps/cli/src/models/streaming.rs`, `apps/cli/src/tui/tui_app.rs` | In the TUI, when a local Ollama model's per-turn `/api/show` tool-support probe returned `Ok(false)` OR hit a transient `Err` (Ollama busy/loading, connection reset), `streaming.rs` set `effective_tools = None` and only `eprintln!`'d behind `!tui_active()` — so in the full-screen TUI the tool-drop was completely invisible: the model replied with prose describing what it _would_ do and nothing happened, looking like a normal successful answer. The `Err` arm was the sharp case (a single flaky probe stripped Read/Write/Bash). Fix: (1) a TUI notice queue (`push_tui_notice`/`drain_tui_notices` in `tui/mod.rs`) that lower layers push to and the event loop drains into the transcript as a system message; (2) `notify_tools_dropped` routes to the queue in TUI mode (stderr otherwise); (3) a per-model `OLLAMA_TOOL_SUPPORT` cache so a transient `Err` falls back to the last successful capability check instead of stripping tools the model supports. | Manual tmux: `agi -m tinyllama:latest -p ollama` (no tool support) → sending a message now shows `Local model 'tinyllama:latest': does not advertise tool support. Running this turn without tools.` in the transcript (was silent). 1699 lib tests pass. |

| SSRF-DNS-01 | Low | Tracked | Security/platform | `crates/agiworkforce-network-proxy/src/runtime.rs:702-722` | `host_resolves_to_non_public_ip` returns false (treat as public, do not block) when DNS resolution fails or times out (line 712). This is a DELIBERATE, commented fail-OPEN choice favoring availability, with the allow/deny list as the primary control. It leaves a narrow TOCTOU window: the SSRF check and the later connect() resolve DNS separately, so a host that fails the check's lookup but resolves to a private IP at connect time could reach internal addresses (DNS rebinding). Not a clear bug — a security-vs-availability tradeoff, hence Low. | DECISION (security-lead sign-off, overrides a deliberate choice): in strict mode (allow_local_binding=false) fail CLOSED on resolver failure, OR re-resolve + pin the IP through to connect() to close the TOCTOU without the availability cost. Add a test for the DNS-failure branch. |

| EXEC-POLICY-DUP-01 | Medium | Tracked | CLI + Rust platform | `apps/cli/src/exec_policy.rs`, `crates/agiworkforce-execpolicy` | Two divergent exec-policy (command-execution safety) engines. VERIFIED: the shipping CLI uses its own in-app `apps/cli/src/exec_policy.rs` (`ExecPolicy::load`/`evaluate`, 220 LOC) and does NOT depend on the crate; `crates/agiworkforce-execpolicy` is consumed only by `agiworkforce-protocol` (src/models.rs), not the CLI. So command-safety rules can silently diverge between the CLI and the protocol/crate path. Not dead code (both sides have a consumer) — a duplication needing consolidation, not deletion. | DECISION: route the CLI through `agiworkforce-execpolicy::Policy` (one engine) OR document the in-app engine as canonical for the CLI and add a shared rule-fixture parity test. Behaviour change — cargo-verify + review before landing. |

| DESKTOP-TOOLTIMELINE-01 | Medium | Fixed | Desktop chat | `apps/desktop/src/features/chat/__tests__/useTauriStreamListeners.toolTimeline.test.tsx` | 4 tool-timeline tests failed. CORRECTED ROOT CAUSE (the earlier "routing rewrite" hypothesis was wrong — useTauriStreamListeners.ts was unchanged since 2026-05-21): a test-harness environment gap, NOT a routing change and NOT a product bug. In jsdom there are no Tauri globals → `runtimeEnvironment.isTauri=false` → `supportsLocalAppMode=false` → `appModeStore` initializes to `cloud`. The tests silently ran in cloud mode, where `createConversation()` fires an async `createCloudConversation()` that rejects (no network); its `.catch()` rollback wipes `messagesByConversation`+`messages` during each test's `await waitFor(...)`, so every stream handler found no target message and wrote nothing → assertions saw `undefined`. The AppMode→PrivacyMode trust-boundary refactor wired initial mode to `supportsLocalAppMode` but the test's tauri-mock (`lib/tauri-mock` isTauri:true) is not read by `runtimeEnvironment`. FIXED (test-only, via desktop-engineer): set `mode:'local'` in beforeEach (Tauri desktop always starts local; these tests assert local-session behavior), restore `cloud` in afterEach. No source/assertion changes. SPUN OFF: DESKTOP-CLOUDROLLBACK-01. | `pnpm --filter @agiworkforce/desktop test` → 1837 passed \| 1 skipped (1838), 0 failures |
| DESKTOP-CLOUDROLLBACK-01 | Medium | Fixed (data-loss core) — UI-surface runtime-confirm | Desktop chat | `apps/desktop/src/stores/chat/chatStore.ts` (createConversation cloud path) | Pre-existing UX gap found while fixing DESKTOP-TOOLTIMELINE-01: when `createConversation()` runs in cloud mode and `createCloudConversation()` rejects (network/auth failure), the `.catch()` rollback silently discards the just-added user message and resets `activeConversationId=null` with NO user-visible error. The user's typed message vanishes with no feedback. Not a test bug — a real degrade-silently failure. | DESKTOP-ENGINEER: surface a non-blocking error (toast/inline) and preserve the drafted message on cloud-create failure instead of silently wiping it. **FIXED 2026-06-25 (data-loss core):** the rollback `.catch` (`chatStore.ts` `createConversation` cloud path) now only discards the optimistic conversation when it holds NO user data (`messagesByConversation[id].length === 0`); a conversation the user already messaged is preserved (it keeps the local-id/cloud-mode state it already had during the optimistic window) so the sent message is no longer silently lost. The send's own failure is surfaced by the normal message-error path. Regression test: `chatStore.cloudRollback.test.ts` (2 cases — preserve-with-data, cleanup-when-empty) ✔; desktop tsc 0 errors; existing chatStore suites 46/46. REMAINING (runtime-confirm, owner desktop-engineer): a dedicated non-blocking toast/inline notice for the cloud-sync failure is a UI/UX choice + the downstream behavior of a preserved local-id conversation in cloud mode (does it later sync / display correctly?) needs a `tauri dev` cloud-mode run with a forced cloud-create failure to verify. |
| CI-TIER-SCRIPTS-01 | High | Fixed | Platform / CI | `package.json` (`test:l1`–`test:l4`, `test:security`), `scripts/run-priority-tier.mjs`, `.github/workflows/{test-l1-l2,test-l3-l4}.yml` | The root priority-tier test scripts ran `vitest run --include='**/priority-level-N/**'` from the repo root. FIXED by `scripts/run-priority-tier.mjs`, which discovers which apps own matching tests and runs each through its OWN `test` script (`pnpm --filter <pkg> run test <token>` — vitest for web/extension/extension-vscode, jest for mobile). Now `test:security` runs 563 real security tests (web 406 + extension 131 + mobile 26) and `test:l1` runs 71, all green; empty tiers l2–l4 log a loud ⚠️ and exit 0. NOTE: separate from the logless `startup_failure` red (a crashing step produces step logs); and `test:coverage` retains the same config-less-root flaw but is non-blocking (`pnpm test:coverage \|\| true`). extension-vscode's `priority-level-1/{chat-flows,error-states}` dirs are empty placeholders. | `pnpm test:l1` (71 pass) · `pnpm test:security` (563 pass) · `pnpm test:l3:l4` (empty → exit 0) |

| AUTO-ROUTER-MIGRATION-01 | High | Partially fixed (canonical TS/Rust policy; pseudo-model identities removed; ordered fallbacks emitted; Desktop, CLI/VS Code developer-session, and Chrome managed-chat paths migrated) | Platform / routing | `packages/ai/model-registry/catalog/{models.curation,routing-policies,harnesses}.json`, `packages/ai/model-registry/generated/registry.json`, `packages/ai/routing/src/auto.ts`, `packages/contracts/types/src/model-catalog.ts`, `crates/agiworkforce-model-registry`, `crates/agiworkforce-protocol/src/developer_session.rs`, `apps/{desktop,cli,web,mobile,extension-vscode,extension}` | Auto was represented simultaneously as fake model records, hardcoded TypeScript task maps, provider task maps, and Rust strategies. Auto aliases now exist only as routing-profile/picker policy: all four false `managed_cloud` model records were removed from curation and generated compatibility output, the compiler rejects their reintroduction, and Web explicitly classifies profile selections as managed transport without fabricated model metadata. The normalized registry declares quality profiles and per-tier slot admission, separates intrinsic modalities from harness features, and generates equivalent TypeScript and Rust resolvers. Both resolvers now preserve a still-preferred concrete route for prompt-cache continuity, re-route when the task requires it, and emit registry-ordered cross-provider fallbacks. Desktop chat Auto and CLI `--auto` select concrete eligible registry routes instead of sending pseudo-models. The shared CLI/VS Code app-server protocol now carries a typed per-turn routing task; persisted developer sessions retain profile, concrete model, previous task, and immutable trust mode; VS Code classifies every Auto turn through the canonical classifier; the Rust host re-evaluates every typed turn and installs direct fallbacks only for BYOK. The interactive CLI (TUI and classic REPL) now mirrors that path (fixed 2026-07-16): `--auto` launches classify the launch prompt through the CLI's faithful port of the canonical classifier (`apps/cli/src/routing/classify.rs`, taxonomy changes land in `packages/ai/routing/src/classify.ts` first) instead of a hardcoded Coding task, seed the session with the resolved route plus account tier, and every subsequent turn re-classifies and re-resolves with previous-route/previous-task continuity in `AgentSession::send` — Managed turns keep `Provider::ManagedCloud` with no direct upstream fallbacks, BYOK turns install the resolver's fallback chain. Managed execution keeps `Provider::ManagedCloud`, visible provenance, and its AGI-host/JWT boundary; Managed provider failover correctly remains a gateway responsibility rather than leaking upstream IDs into a local direct-provider chain. Chrome migrated (verified 2026-07-16): the typed `CHAT_MESSAGE` carries the visible picker selection plus Quick override and route/task continuity from the side panel through the background worker into the privileged managed handler, every Auto turn classifies through the canonical classifier into the `chrome/managed-chat` managed_cloud runtime profile, unknown selections and unadmitted profiles fail closed, and background chat paths are Managed Cloud only — the desktop bridge carries pairing/automation, never inference, and the stale comments describing a localhost-bridge chat fallback chain were corrected; wire-carriage and cloud-only regression pins added. Migration remains incomplete: Web and Mobile use the canonical resolver but not every entry path supplies previous-route/previous-task continuity; some runtime-profile implementation states lag the verified surface wiring; ; the Managed gateway now executes an advisory header-carried fallback plan (x-agi-fallback-models, validated + tier-rechecked per attempt, pre-first-byte only, never for explicit selections — 2026-07-17) but NO CLIENT EMITS the header yet (follow-up: surfaces forward resolveAutoRoute().fallbacks). Shared UI adapters also still contain profile presentation metadata that must not be mistaken for intrinsic model capability. Until those consumers migrate and provider contract tests prove each harness, execution can still diverge from policy. | Current focused proof: `@agiworkforce/routing` 244/244 plus typecheck/lint; Rust registry Auto 20/20 plus `cargo check`; developer-session protocol 6/6 plus `cargo check`; CLI library 1675 passed, 0 failed, 1 ignored plus `cargo check` (2026-07-16: includes 7 new pins — canonical-taxonomy classifier parity, untyped-turn-not-Coding, and per-turn re-resolution feeding the resolver with continuity); VS Code per-turn routing/chat entry points 30/30 plus typecheck/lint. Earlier catalog, command-registry, Desktop, Web, Mobile, Chrome, and shared-chat evidence remains applicable. Live Managed inference and provider failure rotation are not verified because no production credentials were used. Chrome proof: model-carriage and cloud-only pins in `apps/extension/__tests__/chat-model-carriage.test.ts`; full extension suite 1102/1102 plus typecheck, lint, and check:no-cloud-ipc (2026-07-16). Remaining proof requires continuity state on remaining consumer paths, Managed-gateway fallback execution, per-surface harness admission, and end-to-end provider/tool tests. |

Webhook content is untrusted even though it is prompt-delimited, so this was an unsafe automation boundary. | Auto profiles are now detected from the generated Rust policy and must resolve to a concrete managed-cloud route before hooks/session transport; Local and credential-blind BYOK Auto triggers fail closed with an actionable concrete-model requirement. Live managed daemon inference remains unverified without a production credential. |

| DESKTOP-CHAT-SILENT-FAIL-01 | Critical | Fixed | Desktop Rust / chat + telemetry | `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sys/telemetry/{tracing.rs,mod.rs}`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sys/commands/chat/{types.rs,send_message_setup.rs}`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sys/commands/llm.rs`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/core/llm/providers/ollama.rs` | Three compounding bugs made every first Local-mode chat send in a new conversation fail with no visible error ("user message shows, assistant never replies"), reproduced end-to-end via a real native WebDriver session (see `apps/desktop/wdio/`). (1) `CreateConversationRequest.user_id` had no `#[serde(alias = "userId")]`, so `chat_create_conversation` failed IPC deserialization ("missing field `user_id`") before `chat_send_message` was ever invoked — silent because the caught error surfaces only as an internal stream-error chunk, not a toast. (2) `llm_configure_provider("ollama", ...)` accepted any string as `base_url` with zero validation; a corrupted value degrades three call-layers later into `OllamaProvider::is_available()`'s generic `reqwest::Error{Builder, RelativeUrlWithoutBase}` → `false`, which the router reports as the equally-generic "No local model provider is reachable" — nothing links the failure back to the bad setting. (3) SEPARATE AND MUCH LARGER: `init_tracing()`'s two `tracing_appender::non_blocking` `WorkerGuard`s were function-local and dropped the instant it returned, silently killing both flush threads — every `tracing::` call anywhere in the app, at any level, for the rest of the process lifetime, was a no-op (only the two `info!` calls made before the guards dropped ever reached the log file/stdout). This masked (2) for the entire debugging session — deep `tracing::warn!`/`info!` diagnostics never appeared despite `RUST_LOG=debug`, while `eprintln!` in the same spots worked immediately. If you add `tracing::` diagnostics anywhere in this codebase and they don't show up in `~/Library/Application Support/agiworkforce/logs/`, suspect this exact class of bug before assuming your directive/target string is wrong — verify with a raw `eprintln!` first. FIXED: added the missing alias; added URL scheme validation in `llm_configure_provider`; `init_tracing` now returns both `WorkerGuard`s, held by `TelemetryGuard` for the process lifetime (already kept alive in `lib.rs` via `let _telemetry_guard = telemetry::init()?`). | `cargo build`/`cargo check --release`/`--features sentry` all clean; `pnpm typecheck` clean; empirical WebDriver repro: assistant reply now renders end-to-end; log file went from 2 lines/launch to 227 lines after one send, with deep `tracing::info!` calls now appearing in both the JSON log file and stdout. |

| DESKTOP-CLI-HARNESS-FRAGMENTATION-01 | High | Tracked | Desktop + CLI Rust | `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/{core/agi,core/mcp,core/llm}`, `apps/cli/src/{agent,agents.rs,subagent.rs,subagent_v2.rs}`, `crates/agiworkforce-sandbox-policy` | Full-scope version of `EXEC-POLICY-DUP-01` (that entry covers only command-exec-safety policy; this covers the whole harness). Confirmed via a Goose-harness comparison (`docs/architecture-review/AGI_WORKFORCE_VS_GOOSE_HARNESS.md`): Desktop and CLI share exactly one Cargo path dependency (`agiworkforce-sandbox-policy`) — the agent/tool-execution loop, MCP client (~11.5K lines hand-rolled in Desktop's `core/mcp/` vs ~5.7K in CLI), and LLM provider clients are each independently implemented twice, with no shared harness crate. Contrast: Goose's `crates/goose::Agent` is the single core reused as-is by both `goose-cli` (in-process) and `goose-server` (HTTP, called by its Electron UI) — one engine, two thin clients. AGI Workforce's fragmentation is also the structural reason `DESKTOP-CHAT-SILENT-FAIL-01`'s agent-mode routing bug could exist at all: with two independent execution engines (`spawn_streaming_chat` vs `spawn_streaming_agent` in Desktop), a mis-set flag can silently pick the wrong one; a single-engine design structurally can't have that failure mode. Also separately: `core/mcp/` (~24K lines total incl. `sys/commands/mcp*.rs`) hand-rolls the MCP wire protocol/transport with zero use of the official `rmcp` SDK — see `docs/architecture-review/BUILD_VS_BUY_OSS_ADOPTION.md` for the adoption plan. | Not yet actioned — this is a consolidation/adoption decision requiring founder sign-off on scope and timeline, not a mechanical fix. See the two architecture-review docs above for the concrete plans. |

| DESKTOP-AGI-LOOP-VERIFICATION-01 | High | Tracked | Desktop Rust + Desktop UI | `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/core/agi/{core.rs,orchestrator.rs}`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sys/commands/chat/{agent_mode.rs,send_message_setup.rs,send_message_execution.rs}`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sys/commands/agi.rs`, `apps/desktop/src/{runtime/TauriRuntime.ts,features/agi/*,features/background-tasks/*,features/chat/index.tsx,stores/chat/agentStore.ts}`, `apps/desktop/e2e/agi-safety.spec.ts` (deleted, commit `5573cbde7`) | Verification-loop audit of the two engines flagged by `DESKTOP-CLI-HARNESS-FRAGMENTATION-01`. (1) Main chat loop (`send_message_execution.rs` `spawn_streaming_chat`/`run_nonstreaming_chat`, the path virtually every chat send hits): tool failures ARE fed back to the LLM as `role:"tool"` messages so it can self-correct, with real caps (`max_streaming_tool_iterations`/`loop_max_secs`; non-streaming `max_tool_iterations=25`) — no consecutive-failure-abandon concept, but iteration + wall-clock caps are live by default. (2) `AGICore::achieve_goal` (`core.rs:711-1223`), reached only when `spawn_streaming_agent`/`run_nonstreaming_agent` fire — verified the Goose-comparison doc's claim byte-for-byte against source: `goal_iteration_limit()` caps at 1000 (`core.rs:37-51,720`) AND an absolute 300s wall-clock timeout checked every iteration (`core.rs:721-722,750-766`), both real. Consecutive-failure abandon (`MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES=3`, `core.rs:726`) is gated behind the reflection engine's own `insight.assessment.goal_achievable==false` verdict (`core.rs:1146-1164`); the non-reflection else-branch (`core.rs:1188-1195`) computes `consecutive_failures` but never checks it, and is dead code in practice because both `AGICore` constructors always build `reflection_engine` as `Some(...)` (`core.rs:200,291`) — so plain N-strikes abandonment never fires, only the LLM-judged (potentially optimistic) reflection verdict does. Reachability: this session's `TauriRuntime.ts` fix (commit `d3bf6f2ed`) stopped forwarding the composer's `agentMode` chip value to `enable_agent_mode` at all; confirmed this does NOT orphan `AGICore` — for an explicit model selection, `resolve_request_flags` (`send_message_setup.rs:77-80`) now always resolves `agent_mode=false`, so `AGICore` is reached only through `detect_agent_mode`'s intent heuristic (`agent_mode.rs`) on "auto" model routing plus Accessibility permission plus a working `AutomationService` — narrow but real, unlike the confirmed-orphaned `AgiWorkHome.tsx`. Deleted-test gap: `agi-safety.spec.ts` (removed this session, commit `5573cbde7`) expected a full "AGI Goals" dashboard (iteration counter, timeout warning, abandoned/unachievable status, approve/reject workflow, pause/resume, reflection panel) reached via nav — confirmed via the removal commit's own `git log -S` that this UI never shipped. The individual pieces DO exist and work (`features/agi/{AgentTaskPanel,IterationProgressPanel,AgentTaskMonitor}.tsx`, consuming the real `agi:goal:*`/`agent:plan_update` events `core.rs` emits) but are wired only into the orphaned legacy `features/chat/index.tsx` (via `AppLayout.tsx`/`ChatStream.tsx`) that nothing in `App.tsx` imports — the shipping v3 shell (`DesktopShellV3`) and `ChatInterface` both come from `@agiworkforce/unified-chat`, whose own `ChatStream.tsx` comments `IterationProgressPanel ... → deferred`. So the shipping UI has zero visibility into iteration count, timeout proximity, or abandonment for any agent-mode run a user does trigger. Cancellation is also broken end-to-end for this loop: `AgentOrchestrator::cancel_agent` (`orchestrator.rs:363-394`, exposed as the `cancel_agent`/`orchestrator_cancel_agent` Tauri commands, `sys/commands/agi.rs:472-484,691-703`, called from `stores/chat/agentStore.ts`) only calls `agent.core.stop()`, which sets `AGICore::stop_signal` (`core.rs:1356-1358`) — a flag read only by `AGICore::start()`'s separate continuous-loop method (`core.rs:351`), never by `achieve_goal()` (the method actually spawned per-goal by `submit_goal`, `core.rs:416-420`). `achieve_goal()`'s real cancellation hook is `AGICore::cancel_goal(goal_id)` (`core.rs:1364-1394`, sets `cancellation_requested` and aborts the `JoinHandle`) — correct and working, but never called by `cancel_agent`. Net effect: "cancelling" an agent marks it Failed in the orchestrator's own bookkeeping while the real spawned `achieve_goal` task keeps running to its own caps (1000 iterations/300s, with real tool side effects) unseen and unstoppable. `AgentOrchestrator::process_instruction`'s separate 120s polling wrapper (`orchestrator.rs:664-727`) has the identical bug: on its own timeout it does `self.agents.lock().await.remove(&agent_id)` without ever calling `cancel_goal`, so a user told "Task execution timed out after 120 seconds" can have a zombie goal still executing tools. Both broken call sites are themselves currently unreachable from the shipping v3 UI — `cancel_agent`'s only caller (`BackgroundTasksPanel.tsx`) is, like `features/agi/*`, wired solely into the orphaned `features/chat/index.tsx` tree, and the v3 "Stop" button (`TauriRuntime.stopGeneration` → `chat_stop_generation`) only touches chat-stream-stop flags with no effect on an agent-mode goal. Not fixed here: this is cancellation/concurrency semantics on a path with no current live UI trigger, and a symptom of the two-engine split already tracked by `DESKTOP-CLI-HARNESS-FRAGMENTATION-01`; needs a decision on whether to fix `cancel_agent`/`process_instruction` to call `cancel_goal` now or fold it into that consolidation. | No automated test covers this (the only e2e spec that tried was deleted as testing UI that never shipped). Manual trace only — re-verify with `rg -n -e stop_signal -e cancel_goal apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/core/agi/core.rs apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/core/agi/orchestrator.rs` and `rg -n -e cancel_agent -e orchestrator_cancel_agent apps/desktop/src` before acting. |

| DESKTOP-AGENTMODE-GUARDRAIL-SURFACE-01 | Medium | Partially Fixed (persistence + Plan mode UI; the dead AgentModeSwitcher.tsx was deleted in the W8 sweep 2026-07-15 — remaining open item is the Rust-side egress/host-denylist gap) | Desktop Rust + Desktop UI | `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sys/commands/tool_confirmation.rs`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/core/llm/tool_executor/mod.rs`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sys/security/tool_guard.rs`, `apps/desktop/src/{features/governance/SafetyPolicies.tsx,features/chat/AgentModeSwitcher.tsx,lib/egressGuard.ts,stores/settingsStore.ts}` | **UPDATE (same day):** the permissive-on-restart bug and the missing Plan-mode UI (both described below) are FIXED, commit `c51a5e0f1`. `ToolConfirmationState::new_with_db` now loads `agent_mode`/`auto_approve_all` from the `settings_v2` table (`SettingCategory::Security`, same table the rest of the app's security settings use); `set_agent_mode`/`set_auto_approve_all` persist on every change; a missing row/bad value fails closed to `Safe`/`false`, never the compiled `Build` default. `SafetyPolicies.tsx` now models all 4 `AgentMode` variants including `Plan`. A SECOND, independent instance of the same restart-reset bug was found on the frontend during this fix (via the agent's own `advisor()` review, not the original task framing): `settingsStore.ts`'s `loadSettings()` unconditionally pushed its own stale `chatPreferences.agentMode ?? 'build'` default to the backend on every app load, which — since `SafetyPolicies.tsx` writes mode via a direct `invoke('set_agent_mode', ...)` that never updates this Zustand store — would silently clobber whatever the backend had just correctly restored, reproducing the bug via a second path even with the Rust fix in place. Also fixed: `loadSettings()` now hydrates from `get_agent_mode`/`get_auto_approve_all` instead of pushing a default; `saveSettings()` no longer pushes these two fields at all (redundant with the two real write paths, and was the actual clobber vector). 7 new Rust tests (`agent_mode_persistence_tests`) + new frontend tests pinning "backend wins over stale frontend default." `AgentModeSwitcher.tsx` confirmed (via `git log -S`) extracted from the composer toolbar and never re-wired, with a narrower feature set than `SafetyPolicies.tsx` (no auto-approve toggle, no per-tool table) — not a clean drop-in swap, left as dead code, not deleted. **STILL OPEN, not touched by this fix:** the egress-guard Rust-tool-executor gap (below) and the `AgentModeSwitcher.tsx` dead-code decision. Original audit follows. Verified the `AgentMode` enum (`Safe`/`Plan`/`Build`\[default\]/`Autopilot`, `tool_confirmation.rs:69-75`) IS live on the real tool-execution path and was NOT disconnected by this session's `d3bf6f2ed` fix — the two "agent mode" concepts are unrelated and were never wired together, so there is no regression here. `ToolExecutor::check_tool_confirmation` (`tool_executor/mod.rs:2053`, reached via `execute_tool_call` → `chat/tools.rs::execute_chat_tool` → `execute_chat_tool_with_timeout` → `execute_tool_calls_batch`, the actual dispatcher for both `spawn_streaming_chat` and `run_nonstreaming_chat`, i.e. the default chat path) calls `request_tool_confirmation`, whose first gate is `is_tool_permitted_for_mode(tool_name, current_mode)` (`tool_confirmation.rs:1100-1125`) — real and enforced on every non-Safe-tier tool call. `d3bf6f2ed` only stopped forwarding the AgentControl composer chip's separate `ask`/`auto`/`plan`/`bypass` value onto `enable_agent_mode` (a chat-request flag gating whether `AGICore`/`AgentOrchestrator` runs at all — see `DESKTOP-AGI-LOOP-VERIFICATION-01`); per the commit's own comment that value was a tool-confirmation-permission-style label being wrongly read as an "activate agent mode" switch, and it never touched `ToolConfirmationState::agent_mode`. Gaps found in the `AgentMode` UI surface itself, independent of that fix: (1) `SafetyPolicies.tsx` (`features/governance/SafetyPolicies.tsx:26,55,98`), the only reachable control (Settings → Privacy/Governance), models `AgentMode` as `'safe'` / `'build'` / `'autopilot'` only — `Plan` (a real, coded, gate-affecting variant with its own read-only allowlist, `tool_confirmation.rs:70,197-221`) is absent from every reachable UI. (2) `AgentModeSwitcher.tsx`, the one component that does model all 4 variants correctly, is dead code — not imported outside its own file and tests. (3) `ToolConfirmationState::agent_mode` and `auto_approve_all` are in-memory only (`Arc<Mutex<AgentMode>>`/`AtomicBool`) and are never restored in `new_with_db` (`tool_confirmation.rs:113-125`, which loads only `remembered_choices` from SQLite) — both silently reset to their permissive defaults (`Build`, `false`) on every app relaunch with no persistence and no warning. Egress guard: confirmed `apps/desktop/src/lib/egressGuard.ts` is exactly what its own header states — a WebView/TS-layer `fetch()` wrapper covering roughly 8 known frontend call sites (cloud sync, chat persistence, Stripe checkout, account auth, voice transcription, share dialog). It does not and cannot cover Rust-side tool executors (`core/agi/executors/*`, `core/llm/tool_executor`), which issue `reqwest` directly; no equivalent Rust-side "our-cloud host" denylist exists for tool calls. No current executor hardcodes an AGI-Workforce-owned endpoint (verified via grep — only cosmetic git-signature/test-fixture references to `agiworkforce.com`), and generic network tools (`api_call`, `browser_navigate`, etc.) go through `tool_guard.rs`'s `validate_url` plus safety-tier confirmation (unregistered tools default to `RequiresConfirmation`, `tool_guard.rs:2680-2682`) rather than a host denylist — so a Local-mode tool call could in principle be pointed at `agiworkforce.com`/`vercel.app`/etc. by a malicious skill or MCP server, and nothing blocks it by hostname; the only protection is the standard per-call confirmation dialog (which does show the destination) for any tool above Safe tier. | Manual code trace only; no automated test added. Re-verify with `rg -n -e is_tool_permitted_for_mode -e request_tool_confirmation apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/core/llm/tool_executor/mod.rs` and `rg -n AgentMode apps/desktop/src/features/governance/SafetyPolicies.tsx`. |

| DESKTOP-BYOK-OPENROUTER-VERIFY-01 | Critical | Fixed (this session) | Desktop | `apps/desktop/src/features/onboarding/OnboardingWizard.tsx` (`detectProvider`, `handleByokSubmit`), `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs` (`setup()`, BYOK rehydration call), `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sys/commands/llm.rs` (`rehydrate_byok_providers`, `llm_list_openrouter_models`), `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/core/llm/providers/direct_api_provider.rs` (`apply_auth`) | Tier-1 checklist "verify and complete OpenRouter support" pass. OpenRouter's own wiring (`Provider::OpenRouter`, `Provider::from_string("open_router"\|"openrouter"\|"open-router")`, `DirectApiProvider` base URL/auth/SSE via the OpenAI-compatible adapter, `save_api_key`→router registration from Settings → Models & Keys) was already correct and is unchanged. Found and fixed two bugs in the SHARED BYOK machinery that made OpenRouter (and every other BYOK provider) not actually usable end-to-end for a real user, both matching the `DESKTOP-CHAT-SILENT-FAIL-01` bug class (key accepted with no error, chat silently never works): (1) **Onboarding never registers the provider.** `OnboardingWizard.tsx`'s BYOK paste flow (`handleByokSubmit`) called `secret_manager_set(detected.secretKey, apiKey)` — e.g. `open_router_api_key` — which writes into `SecretManager`'s unrelated `settings` table (used only for JWT/DB-encryption keys and per-tool credentials like Research Settings' `perplexity_api_key`, confirmed still consumed correctly by `search_executor.rs` — NOT the same bug, left untouched). Nothing in the chat/provider-routing path (`save_api_key`/`llm_configure_provider`/`retrieve_api_key`, all keyed on `settings_v2`'s `api_key_<provider>` rows) ever reads that store back, so `router.has_provider(OpenRouter)` stays false forever and BYOK chat can't work — for all 6 auto-detected providers (anthropic/google/xai/perplexity/`open_router`/openai), not just OpenRouter. Fixed: `detectProvider` now returns the canonical `Provider::from_string`-compatible id, and `handleByokSubmit` calls `save_api_key` (same path Settings → Models & Keys uses), which registers the provider on the router immediately. (2) **BYOK registration doesn't survive an app restart.** `save_api_key`/`llm_configure_provider` only add a provider to the in-memory `LLMRouter` at the moment a key is saved; the frontend's settings-rehydration callback (`stores/settingsStore.ts`) only re-registers the three local runtimes (Ollama/LM Studio/llama.cpp) on launch, never BYOK cloud providers — confirmed via full-repo grep, no other rehydration path exists. So every configured BYOK provider (OpenRouter included) reported `configured: false` after every app restart even though its key was safely stored (encrypted) in `settings_v2`. Fixed: added `rehydrate_byok_providers()` (reads all `api_key_<provider>` rows from `settings_v2`, decrypts via the existing `retrieve_api_key` helper, re-registers each as a `DirectApiProvider`), called once from `lib.rs`'s `setup()` after the LLM router and master-password encryption state exist. Also completed the product gap the task flagged: OpenRouter's catalog (hundreds of models) was represented by only 4 hand-curated free-tier entries in `models.curation.json` — added `llm_list_openrouter_models` (live `GET https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/models`, no auth header needed — confirmed against the already-shipped reference `apps/cli/src/models/openrouter_models.rs`, which calls the same endpoint unauthenticated) mirroring the LM Studio/llama.cpp `/v1/models` dynamic-listing pattern from commit `95b15e61b`; wired into `llm_get_available_models`, gated on `router.has_provider(OpenRouter)`, de-duplicated against the curated entries, filtering out non-chat media-output models (image/audio/video). Also added OpenRouter's recommended `HTTP-Referer`/`X-Title` attribution headers to `apply_auth` (mirrors `packages/ai/providers/openrouter/src/index.ts`'s existing `OPENROUTER_SITE_URL`/`OPENROUTER_APP_TITLE`, previously desktop-only was missing these). Verified NOT a bug (checked because the task called out this exact risk class): the OpenAI-compatible SSE parser already correctly ignores OpenRouter's `: OPENROUTER PROCESSING`-style comment keep-alives (`SseStreamParser::is_keepalive_event` checks for lines starting with `:` before `parse_openai_sse` ever sees them) and uses the correct `\n\n` delimiter (`models.curation.json`'s `open_router.sseDelimiter`) — no OpenRouter-specific streaming quirk found. | `cargo check`/`cargo check --release`/`cargo build` all clean; `pnpm typecheck` clean; new Rust unit tests (8 in `sys::commands::llm`, 1 in `direct_api_provider`) pass; new `OnboardingWizard.test.tsx` (2 tests) pass; full `cargo test -p agiworkforce-desktop --lib core::llm::` reruns clean except 4 pre-existing, unrelated failures in `h13_resolve_model_for_strategy` matching the already-tracked `DESKTOP-ECONOMY-ROUTING-01` regression (confirmed via `git diff --stat` showing zero uncommitted changes to `models.json`/`llm_router.rs` — not caused by this session's diff). NOT verified: a live end-to-end send with a real OpenRouter API key (no key available in this sandbox) — failure-mode correctness for missing/invalid keys was verified by source trace (`is_configured()`/`llm_configure_provider`'s explicit "No API key provided" error) and existing test coverage instead. UPDATE (same session): also found and fixed a picker-scale UX gap the live catalog exposed — `apps/desktop/src/features/v3/ModelPopover.tsx`'s "Local / BYOK" list (the shipping v3 shell's model picker) rendered `storeModels` as a flat, unbounded list of buttons with no search and no scroll bound; OpenRouter's live catalog (hundreds of models, all flagged `isByok` per `App.tsx`'s `isByok` computation) would have overflowed the dropdown off-screen with no way to find a specific model — defeating the reason for adding live listing in the first place. Fixed: added a filter input (shown once the list exceeds 8 models) and a `max-h-80 overflow-y-auto` scroll bound; verified via 3 new tests in `ModelPopover.test.tsx` (50-model list stays reachable via scroll, filter narrows correctly, small local-only lists are unaffected/no filter box shown). |
