| MCP-APPROVAL-RESUME | High | Fixed (2026-07-09) | Web lead | apps/web/app/api/llm/v1/chat/completions/approve/route.ts, apps/web/app/api/llm/v1/chat/completions/lib/tool-loop.ts, apps/web/lib/hooks/useChatStream.ts, apps/web/features/chat/components/messages/ToolTimeline.tsx, apps/web/features/chat/components/messages/MessageBubble.tsx, apps/web/features/chat/pages/WebChatPage.tsx | ORIGINAL GAP: manual-mode (the default) MCP-class tools (operator MCP + user connectors) never executed end-to-end on web. The v1 tool-loop fail-closed to awaiting_approval, emitted an x_tool_approval_request SSE event, and returned; ToolCallCard.onApprove was wired to nothing and no resume endpoint existed. Only E2B tools ran (they take the auto path). FIXED with a STATELESS, client-driven resume. SUSPEND/RESUME DATA-FLOW: the manual suspend appends the assistant tool_call turn to the in-memory thread, emits one x_tool_approval_request per pending call, emits [DONE], and returns — NO server-side loop state is persisted. The client (useChatStream.consumeAssistantStream, a shared SSE consumer extracted from sendMessage) parses x_tool_approval_request into awaiting_approval tool cards and registers the suspended turn (prior thread + pending calls) in a module-level registry keyed by assistant message id. On approve-or-reject, once EVERY pending call is decided, the client POSTs the full thread INCLUDING the reconstructed assistant tool_call turn plus a tool_approvals array to the NEW POST /api/llm/v1/chat/completions/approve endpoint, and streams the continuation into the same assistant message. That endpoint re-runs the SAME auth + managed-compute gate + processRequest (credit reservation, model resolution) as the main route, re-loads the per-request tool catalog (operator MCP + the user's connected connectors), and drives runToolLoop with options.resume. runToolLoop's resume preamble executes ONLY approved calls whose tool_call_id matches a pending call, appends a denial tool result for rejected-or-undecided ones, then continues the normal loop to a final answer (a further tool suspends again — recursive approval). SECURITY (fail-closed, per-tool, server-verified): a tool runs ONLY when the resume carries an explicit approved decision for a tool_call_id ACTUALLY PENDING in the replayed assistant turn — the approve route rejects any mismatched-or-forged id with a 400 BEFORE any credit reservation or stream (executes nothing), and the loop preamble re-checks and refuses the whole resume on a non-pending id. Approved calls must ALSO be present in the freshly-loaded per-request mcpTools catalog (a hallucinated qualified name is NOT executed, it gets an error tool-result). Every runtime guard is re-run on the resumed execution — connector per-user re-gate, SSRF DNS-resolution check, unknown-server rejection — all inside runMcpTool and makeUserConnectorExecutor, which the resume threads exactly as the main route does. Undecided calls default to denial (the fail-closed default is not weakened). BYTE-STABLE PUBLIC WIRE: the resume lives on a SEPARATE subpath endpoint that is NOT covered by the vercel.json /v1/chat/completions rewrite, the main route is untouched, and the auto-mode execution was refactored into a shared generator (runAndStreamToolCalls) emitting identical SSE bytes — the full v1 chat/completions byte-parity suite stays green. EXTENDED-THINKING HANDLING (degraded, not refused): a suspended Anthropic turn with extended thinking carries a SIGNED thinking block Anthropic requires replayed before the tool_use blocks (see TOOLLOOP-ANTHROPIC-THINKING-CONTINUITY-01 and request-processor's no-thinking-on-wire invariant); that signature is server-only and cannot cross a stateless resume on the client wire. Instead of refusing that config (which — because approvalMode is manual whenever hasMcpTools, and any GitHub-connected user has connector tools — would permanently block approval for GitHub-connected users on thinking-enabled Claude models), the approve endpoint FORCES thinking OFF on the resume continuation (a post-tool answer needs no extended thinking). With thinking disabled there is no signed-block-before-tool_use requirement, so the continuation COMPLETES (degraded only in that this one continuation turn has no extended thinking) rather than being rejected. The web client already omits thinking on resume; the server strip makes it robust for any caller. Carrying signed thinking across resume WOULD require server-side suspended-loop state, deliberately rejected here to keep the route serverless-stateless. | apps/web tsc 0; full apps/web suite 3786 passed (262 files, up from 3757); check:boundaries + check:llm-failures pass. Server loop: lib/tool-loop.resume.test.ts (6 — manual suspend surfaces the approval event and executes nothing; a valid approval executes the tool through the connector executor and continues to a final answer; a mismatched-or-forged tool_call_id executes nothing; reject appends a denial and the model continues; approved-but-not-offered fails closed with no execution; an Anthropic resume runs normally with no special case since the endpoint drops thinking). Route boundary: approve/route.test.ts (5 — forged id 400 before processRequest, no-suspended-turn 400, malformed-body 400, thinking forced off on the resumed processed, a valid pending approval passes the boundary to processRequest). Client: useChatStream.approval.test.tsx (4 — suspend surfaces the awaiting_approval card; approve dispatches the resume to /approve with the reconstructed assistant tool_call turn plus tool_approvals and streams the continuation into the same message; reject sends decision rejected; no resume fires until every pending tool is decided). UI: ToolTimeline.test.tsx (+3 — awaiting_approval auto-expands and renders approve/reject; approve and reject forward the exact tool_call_id). The behavior-preserving SSE-consumer extraction keeps the existing useChatStream (9) and v1 byte-parity/golden suites unchanged. NOT live-verified against a real browser round-trip to a scripted MCP server (no operator MCP endpoint configured in-repo, same environment gap as WEB-CONNECTORS remainder b); the deterministic loop/route/client tests stand as the behavioral proof. |

| GEMINI-FUNCTIONCALL-THOUGHT-SIGNATURE-01 | Medium | Mitigated (2026-07-10) — real-signature continuity open | Web lead | packages/ai/providers/google/src/translate.ts, packages/ai/providers/google/src/stream.ts, packages/ai/provider-protocol/src/openai-wire-compat.ts, apps/web/app/api/llm/v1/chat/completions/lib/tool-loop.ts | Gemini 3 strictly validates thought signatures on replayed functionCall parts: any tool-loop continuation turn (assistant functionCall + functionResponse history) without a thoughtSignature 400s with INVALID_ARGUMENT ("Function call is missing a thought_signature in functionCall parts"), found live 2026-07-10 (gemini-3.5-flash, url_fetch through the agentic loop). Root cause is the Gemini variant of TOOLLOOP-ANTHROPIC-THINKING-CONTINUITY-01: the OpenAI-compatible message thread between loop steps has no slot for Gemini's per-functionCall signature (streamed as part.thoughtSignature; translateGeminiStream/ToolUseBlock never carry it), so the replayed call arrives signature-less. MITIGATION (translate.ts): every replayed tool_use part gets the officially documented injected-call dummy signature "skip_thought_signature_validator" (ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/generate-content/thought-signatures), which skips validation at possible cost of some reasoning continuity — honest per Google docs, not a hack. REAL FIX would thread the true signature: capture part.thoughtSignature on functionCall parts in stream.ts, carry it through StreamChunkToolUseStart + a tool-loop side-channel (like stepSink.thinkingBlocks), and replay it on the exact functionCall part — needs a shared-contract change (packages/contracts/types ToolUseBlock), so it is a contracts-owner decision. | Regression tests: packages/ai/providers/google server-side-tool-invocations.test.ts (replayed tool_use part carries the dummy signature; 6 tests). Live verified 2026-07-10: gemini-3.5-flash with google_search + url_fetch on one request — model called url_fetch, loop executed it, continuation turn accepted (no 400), grounded answer streamed. |

Related honesty bugs fixed in the same pass: POST /api/connectors 501'd even the five local connectors because the UI sends authType pat while the route required literal authType local (Connect was a silent optimistic-revert no-op with no error toast anywhere); operator-mapped remote MCP connectors were unreachable because the enablement row they are gated on could never be created (501); GitHub showed as never-connected in the directory because GET only read user_connectors while chat tools run off github_installations; the directory advertised 5 fabricated GitHub capabilities (Push code etc.) when the real tool set is 3 (PR diff, issue comment, PR review); the OAuth Coming-soon tier deep-linked to /pricing implying a plan gate that does not exist. FIXES: new GET /api/github/install/start (auth, sets state cookie, 302 to github.com/apps/GITHUB_APP_SLUG/installations/new, 503-redirect when slug unset); callback redirects to /connectors with query flags surfaced as toasts; offering gate = installation row + mintable app creds (isGitHubAppConfigured), not the lazily-cached token; GET /api/connectors returns github-app-derived connected state plus an `available` capability list so the UI renders Connect vs Coming-soon from deployment truth; POST accepts local ids (classified by id, stored auth_type local) and operator-mapped ids, 409s github with installStartPath, 501s the rest; DELETE github unlinks the user's installations; use-connectors surfaces server error messages via toasts and follows the 409 install redirect; ConnectorsPage consolidated onto the shared hook (dedupe/429 cache restored), tools/action counts render only for live connectors, GitHub detail gets Manage-on-GitHub. GUARD: when adding any multi-hop auth flow, walk it end-to-end on paper — initiator sets what the callback checks, callback lands on a route that exists, and the runtime gate keys on a signal the flow actually produces. | apps/web tsc 0 (typecheck run after each edit batch). Not live-verified against a real GitHub App install (needs GITHUB_APP_SLUG + app credentials in env); flow verified by code-path walkthrough end-to-end. Suites not run per session constraint (no testing) — run apps/web vitest + a live install round-trip in a supervised session. |

Net: API keys work on GET/non-CSRF routes but cannot call the main API they exist for. Exploiting it requires the caller to already hold a valid credential, so it is a correctness/least-surprise defect rather than an open forgery hole. Final-shape fix is in api-auth.ts, not the CSRF layer: any request presenting a Bearer header should never silently fall back to cookie-session identity (reject or prefer the bearer, decided once, tested). | New tests must pin: bearer+cookie requests resolve identity from the bearer (or reject), never silently from the cookie; existing single-credential paths byte-unchanged. The live `useVoiceHotkey` listens only on `document`, so it works only while AGI's webview is focused. `voice_stop_global_ptt` changes an atomic flag and detaches the handle, but returning from an `rdev::listen` callback does not terminate the blocking listener; re-enabling can add another listener and both can emit when the shared flag becomes true. The native recorder builds only an `f32` stream without branching on the device's actual sample format, and its “two second” thread-join comment contradicts an unbounded blocking `join()`. Text injection is an unwired Enigo typing call with no original-target pin/revalidation, secure-field refusal, clipboard transaction, focus-race recovery, or transcript recovery. ## Update Rules

- Add a row when a repeated bug class, stale claim, or high-risk open gap is discovered.
- Mark `Fixed` only after naming the PR/commit or verification command.
- Prefer owner roles over individual names until the team is hired.
  | DESKTOP-RUST-ECONOMY-ROUTING-DRIFT-2026-07-10 | Medium | Fixed (2026-07-10) via SSOT-derivation | Desktop lead | apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/core/llm/tests/coderabbit*fix_tests.rs (h13_resolve_model_for_strategy), apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/core/llm/… (LLMRouter::resolve_model_for_strategy), packages/contracts/types/src/models.json | Running the desktop src-tauri suite (`cargo test --lib`, NOT run this session until 2026-07-10 — same "committed but suite unverified" gap as the mobile jest regression) surfaced 5 failures (4345 passed / 5 failed). ONE FIXED same day: the thinking-mode test (see below). The other 4 are model-catalog DRIFT: `LLMRouter::resolve_model_for_strategy(AutoEconomy, …)` now returns `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` for managed-chat-sized tokens where the tests hard-code `gpt-5.4-mini` (rs:134 `left: "gemini-3.1-flash-lite"`), plus large-token/boundary variants that hard-code `gpt-5.4-mini`/`gemini-3.1-pro-preview`. models.json (the SSOT) changed the economy defaults; the tests hard-code the OLD ids — which itself violates the "read model IDs from models.json, never hardcode" rule. FIXED without needing catalog intent: the 4 tests hard-coded model ids (violating "read model ids from models.json, never hardcode"), which is exactly why they drift. Rewrote them to assert the ROUTING DECISION against the SSOT — `assert_eq!(resolve_model_for_strategy(AutoEconomy, N).as_str(), get_task_model(&Provider::ManagedCloud, "chat"|"long_context"))` — pinning that economy-medium→ManagedCloud-chat and economy-large→ManagedCloud-long_context, while the model id floats with models.json. This catches routing-LOGIC regressions (wrong provider/task) but not intentional catalog-value changes, so no catalog-owner guess is required. | Fixed `coderabbit_fix_tests.rs` h13 module (4 assertions → SSOT-derived + imports). Verified: `cargo test --lib h13_resolve_model_for_strategy` → 19 passed / 0 failed. Follow-up DONE same day: migrated the ENTIRE h13 module — the passing sibling routing tests (auto_balanced/premium/small-economy + all boundary tests) also hard-coded ids and would drift identically, so all were converted to `get_task_model(&Provider::X, task)` SSOT-derivation (OpenAI/fast_completion+chat, Anthropic/chat+complex_reasoning, ManagedCloud/chat+long_context). No hard-coded model ids remain in the module; h13 19/19 green. REPEATED BUG CLASS (record for future): routing/model-selection tests that hard-code models.json ids break whenever the catalog changes (hit here on desktop economy + mobile model-picker-cloud-labels) — assert the value-agnostic ROUTING DECISION (provider/task) against the SSOT instead. |

Explicit false still short-circuits BEFORE trigger-word auto-detection, so "ultrathink" never re-enables it — the test's INTENT (explicit-off beats the trigger) holds; thinking is disabled, just as `Enabled(false)` not `None`. ChatInterface renders MessageList passing onArtifactClick + onContinueGeneration but NOT onRetry/onRegenerate, and useChat only returns sendMessage/stopGeneration/continueGeneration/isStreaming — there is no regenerate capability at all, so the assistant ActionBar Regenerate is gated off (absent), not broken. True web parity requires a NEW shared runtime.regenerate(conversationId) capability (drop last assistant turn, re-run last user turn) implemented across Local/Cloud/BYOK runtimes, exposed by useChat, and threaded ChatInterface → MessageList → MessageBubble → ActionBar. This touches the send pipeline across all three runtimes and CANNOT be verified without a running Tauri app + a live/local model, so it is supervised, not an autonomous edit. (2) DEAD LEGACY: apps/desktop/src/features/v3/ActiveChat.tsx (zero importers) and apps/desktop/src/features/chat/ChatStream.tsx both contain mis-wired regenerate — ChatStream wires onRegenerate to startEditingMessage(assistantId, assistantContent) (would dump the assistant reply into an edit box) and both route to editAndRegenerateFromMessage/forkAndRegenerate, which only fork+truncate via the conversation_fork Tauri command and NEVER trigger a new completion (composer submit is the sole path to a new assistant message; the code comment "populate the input and allow the user to send" confirms manual-by-design). These stacks are unmounted → not a live bug, but a maintainability/cleanup candidate and a trap for future work. | Reverse-engineered the full live mount chain (App.tsx → shared ChatInterface) + both dead stacks by import-graph tracing (ActiveChat: 0 importers; ChatInterface: the lazy-mounted surface). Fix + verify: add runtime.regenerate across runtimes, wire onRetry through the shared tree, then drive Regenerate on a signed-in/local-model desktop build via WDIO and confirm a fresh streamed completion replaces the prior assistant turn. Separately: delete/quarantine the dead ChatStream/ActiveChat regenerate stacks once confirmed fully unreferenced. REFINED RECIPE (traced web's working impl 2026-07-10): do NOT invent a novel mechanism — PORT web's proven client-side REPLAY pattern. Web (`apps/web/features/chat/lib/regenerateReplay.ts` + WebChatPage) does: (a) `getRegenerateReplayDecision({userMetadata, assistantMetadata})` gates regenerate — blocks skill-guided turns (`sendReplay.hasSkillInstruction`) and legacy tool-assisted turns lacking replay metadata, each with an honest user message; (b) `replayToSendOptions(sendReplay)` recovers the original webSearch/thinking/codeExecution/styleMode toggles from the USER turn's `sendReplay` metadata; (c) truncate messages after that user turn + re-`sendMessage` with the replayed options. Both helper fns are PURE (no DOM/store deps) → extract them to `packages/ui/unified-chat` (shared, zero-dup) so desktop reuses the SAME gating + option-replay. The remaining runtime-specific piece is only the truncate-then-resend persistence (each runtime must drop-after-user-turn consistently so reload doesn't diverge) + the ActionBar wiring — still Tauri/WDIO-verified, but this converts the task from greenfield to "reuse web's helpers + one persistence method per runtime." |

  | MOBILE-PRESSABLE-CSSINTEROP-FLEXDIR-01 | Medium | Fixed (2026-07-11, uncommitted tree) — round 3: repo-wide sweep done, 6 more live instances found and fixed; round 3.1: XcodeBuildMCP re-verification pass, FileExportButton confirmed live, CollapsibleSources/WebSearchResultCard correctly blocked on WP4 (not a gap), mechanism still NOT fully isolated | Mobile lead | apps/mobile/src/features/settings/cloud-connectors/index.tsx (ConnectorCard), apps/mobile/src/features/settings/cloud-usage/index.tsx (Refresh button), apps/mobile/src/features/chat/components/{CollapsibleSources,ConversationExportSheet,FileExportButton,TaskChips,WebSearchResultCard}.tsx | During the Connectors-screen visual redesign, ConnectorCard rendered visibly wrong (icon stacked above the name, full-width outlined button) although the source JSX correctly set `flexDirection:'row', alignItems:'center', gap:12` on the Pressable's function-style `style={({pressed}) => ({...})}` prop. First suspected as a stale-bundle artifact because Metro was separately, genuinely crashing (`TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'addedFiles')` in `metro/src/node-haste/DependencyGraph.js` `_onHasteChange`, triggered by `react-native-css-interop@0.2.3`'s Metro `onChange` hook — a real, reproducible crash from this exact `react-native-css-interop@0.2.3` + `metro@0.83.7` version pairing, distinct from the layout bug). Isolated the two: killed Metro, restarted clean with `--clear`, confirmed `packager-status:running` with no crash in the log, then reproduced the SAME broken stacked/full-width render by putting the layout properties back on the Pressable's function-style prop on that verified-clean bundler. Moving `flexDirection`/`alignItems`/`gap` onto a plain object-literal-style child `View` fixed that — confirmed by screenshot in both directions on the same clean Metro instance. ROUND 2 (same day): round 1's workaround left `paddingHorizontal`/`paddingVertical` behind on the Pressable's function-style prop (only the properties that visibly broke were moved off; the rest of that prop's contents were never audited). A founder screenshot then showed connector icons clipped ~15-20% on the left edge, cramped row spacing, and full-bleed dividers. Two candidate causes were live simultaneously — paddingHorizontal silently dropped (same mechanism as round 1), or the outer card's `overflow:'hidden'`+`borderRadius` clipping a flush-left icon — and the fix applied (moving ALL styling off the Pressable's function-style prop via its `children`-as-function form instead, onto plain object-literal child Views, plus larger explicit padding) addresses both at once, so it does NOT discriminate between them; do not read round 2 as confirming the padding-drop theory, only as consistent with it. LESSON: when a prop is found to silently drop one property, audit every property on that same prop, not just the one that visibly broke. Net: in this stack (`nativewind@4.2.3` + `react-native-css-interop@0.2.3` wrapping `Pressable`), a function-style `style` callback is unreliable for style resolution generally (confirmed for flexDirection/alignItems, suspected but not isolated for padding) even with a crash-free bundler; this is NOT general React Native behavior (the pattern works in plain RN) and must not be generalized past this nativewind/css-interop pairing. REPEATED BUG CLASS — CONFIRMED, round 3 (2026-07-11): ran the follow-up grep across apps/mobile/src, found 39 Pressables (29 distinct files) using a function-style `style` prop. Triaged by structure rather than fixing blind: DANGEROUS shape is layout properties (flexDirection/alignItems/gap/padding) applied directly on the Pressable's own function-style prop with multiple children laid out by that same prop; SAFE shapes are (a) the Pressable's function-style prop sets only pressed-state `backgroundColor` with the real multi-child layout built as a separately-styled child element, (b) the function-style prop only centers a single child (alignItems+justifyContent without flexDirection risk, since centering one child is direction-independent), or (c) layout comes from a `className` (nativewind) prop with the function-style `style` prop reserved for backgroundColor only — this className pattern was live-verified safe via CommandPalette (typed "/" in composer, icon+label rendered correctly) and is architecturally distinct from the broken mechanism. Found and fixed 6 real instances of the dangerous shape beyond the original connectors one: cloud-usage/index.tsx Refresh button (icon+"Refresh" text were stacked, not visible at a first glance — screenshot re-inspection required, live-verified fixed), chat/components/CollapsibleSources.tsx source-list rows, ConversationExportSheet.tsx export options (live-verified fixed via a real conversation's Share→Export flow), FileExportButton.tsx file-export actions, TaskChips.tsx task-mode chips (component confirmed dead code — zero renders anywhere in the app currently, so this fix has no live effect until something renders it), WebSearchResultCard.tsx search-result rows. CollapsibleSources/FileExportButton/WebSearchResultCard fixes were NOT independently live-verified in round 3 (no reachable conversation in the test account's history had sources, a successfully-harvested exportable file, or real web-search results respectively) — fixed by the same proven pattern and typechecked, but flagged as code-fix-only pending a live message that exercises them. ROUND 3.1 (2026-07-11, same day, via XcodeBuildMCP): FileExportButton is now live-verified — export operates on already-existing message content, so no new model call was needed; long-pressed an existing assistant message → Export Message, and all four rows (Export as PDF, Export as Text, Copy to Clipboard, Share...) rendered icon-beside-label correctly. CollapsibleSources/WebSearchResultCard remain unverified — traced to a precise, confirmed-not-a-bug cause rather than left as an environmental guess: two explicit web-search prompts to GPT-5.4 Mini both got "I don't have access to a live web search tool in this chat," and AddToChatSheet.tsx's own "Web search" toggle never rendered for this model. Read the gating chain instead of trusting the model's self-report as ground truth: AddToChatSheet.tsx:325 only shows the toggle when `FEATURES.webSearch && selectedModelSupportsSearch`, where `selectedModelSupportsSearch` reads `getModelMetadataById(selectedModel)?.capabilities?.search` (AddToChatSheet.tsx:66-73, with its own comment explaining why: "the server silently strips the web_search tool for models whose provider adapter can't wire it up yet (e.g. OpenAI models via the chat-completions endpoint, which rejects the Responses-API-only web_search_preview tool type)"). packages/contracts/types/src/models.json confirms `gpt-5.4-mini` has `"search": false` — deliberately, for exactly that reason. The model's decline was accurate; this is correct, intentional per-model gating, not a bug. Other catalog models (claude-haiku-4.5, sonar, gemini-3.1-pro-preview, etc.) DO have `"search": true` and would exercise these two components, but this session's model-usage rule pins GPT-5.4 Mini specifically for tool-calling tests, so switching models to force a live render was out of scope. Mobile lead independently traced this platform-wide for the founder and confirmed it's broader than one model: every OpenAI model mobile users get (gpt-5-nano, gpt-4.1-nano, gpt-5.4-mini) has `search:false`; only `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` has `search:true` and can search on mobile today. CollapsibleSources/WebSearchResultCard's rendered state is genuinely unreachable for the vast majority of mobile models until WP4 (web-search-as-a-server-tool-for-all-tools-capable-models) ships — this is expected and correctly blocked, not an effort gap; status is "verify when WP4 lands," not "re-check later." The parallel file-creation test ("Run code to write notes.md") also declined ("I can't directly write files to your disk"): `gpt-5.4-mini` DOES have `"codeExecution": true` in the catalog, but AddToChatSheet.tsx's "Run code" toggle additionally requires `codeExecutionDeploymentAvailable` (billing/store.ts:98, sourced honestly from the real `/api/me` `feature_flags.code_execution`, mirroring the server `AGI_E2B_EXECUTION` env var) — that deployment flag was false for this account/session, most likely because `FEATURES.codeExecution` in v1FeatureFlags.ts was only flipped true today (2026-07-11) and the E2B rollout is still in progress; this is a legitimate server-side deployment gate, not client-side breakage (the client reads and honors the real flag correctly). The round-2 connectors fix itself was also independently re-confirmed live in round 3.1 (fresh dark-mode screenshot via XcodeBuildMCP: icons unclipped, rows spacious, dividers correctly inset — unchanged from round 2). ApprovalCard.tsx and CitationChip.tsx use the same className-based SAFE shape as the live-verified CommandPalette, so were left as-is (not independently re-verified — ApprovalCard needs a fresh pending tool-approval, CitationChip needs a message with citations, neither reachable without a new model call). Two low-severity candidates (cloud-usage/index.tsx:189 "View on web" fallback button — currently unreachable, feature-flagged off in this build; settings/common.tsx:145 CloudSyncBlockedBanner "Switch to AGI Cloud" button) have only padding/backgroundColor at risk with a single Text child, so worst case is a slightly cramped button, not a broken layout — left unfixed, not independently verified. LESSON for the visual gate itself: the cloud-usage Refresh button's stacked icon+text was subtle enough (two small elements close together) to be misread as correct on a first glance at the screenshot — re-examine closely, don't skim, especially for compact multi-element rows. | Workaround (round 2, more conservative): put NO `style` prop on Pressable at all; use its `children={(pressed) => ...}` render-prop form and put every real style — layout, padding, and pressed-state background — on plain object-literal-style child Views. Verified via screenshot in both dark and light appearance, matched scroll position, both unconnected and connected-badge states (connectors); Refresh button, Export sheet, and (round 3.1) FileExportButton fixes are all live-verified the same way; CollapsibleSources/WebSearchResultCard/TaskChips fixed and typechecked but not independently live-verified — the first two are blocked specifically by `gpt-5.4-mini` having `capabilities.search: false` in packages/contracts/types/src/models.json (correct, intentional per-model gating per AddToChatSheet.tsx:66-73, not a bug — confirmed by code, not just the model's self-report), confirmed platform-wide (not just this session's model pin): every OpenAI model on mobile has `search:false`, only `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` has `search:true` — so this is correctly blocked on WP4 (web-search-as-a-server-tool-for-all-tools-capable-models) shipping, status is "verify when WP4 lands"; TaskChips is dead code with zero render sites. Repro the triage: `grep -rn "Pressable" apps/mobile/src --include=*.tsx -A5 \| grep -B5 "style={({"` lists all 39 call sites; re-check CollapsibleSources/WebSearchResultCard once WP4 ships, plus the two low-severity/unverified single-child cases and the two className-family cases (ApprovalCard, CitationChip) next time their surfaces are exercised live. |
